This process involves: - Filing an affidavit asserting that the occupant is unauthorized. - Providing evidence that the individual is not a tenant or authorized resident.

Once these conditions are met, law enforcement officers are authorized to remove the squatter without the need for a court order. citeturn0search8 2. **Unlawful Detainer Action:** If immediate removal is not feasible or the situation is complex, property owners may file an unlawful detainer action.

This legal process requires: - Filing a complaint in the circuit court. - Serving the squatter with a summons and complaint. - Obtaining a court order for eviction if the squatter does not vacate voluntarily.

This method is distinct from eviction proceedings, as it pertains to individuals who have no legal right or lease agreement to occupy the property. citeturn0search13 **Public Property:** When it comes to public property, such as parks, sidewalks, or government-owned land, the responsibility for addressing squatters typically falls to local government authorities: 1. **Law Enforcement Agencies:** Local police or sheriff's departments are generally responsible for enforcing laws against unauthorized occupation of public spaces.

They may: - Issue warnings or citations to individuals occupying public property unlawfully. - Remove individuals who refuse to vacate after being notified. 2. **Municipal Responsibilities:** Municipalities are required to take lawful measures to prevent or remove squatters from public property.

For example, a law signed in March 2024 allows property owners to request immediate removal of unauthorized persons by law enforcement, bypassing the traditional court process. citeturn0search14 Given the evolving nature of these laws and the potential for local variations in enforcement, it's advisable to consult with legal counsel or local authorities in Miami-Dade County to ensure compliance with current regulations and procedures. > **User (2025-02-06 18:42:59 UTC):** > > “For example, a law signed in March 2024 allows property owners to request immediate removal of unauthorized persons by law enforcement, bypassing the traditional court process.” That paragraph doesn’t make sense.

(Miami-Dade County, FL) **From:** Joshua Durden Unhoused Individual, System Survivor Email: jojidurde@gmail.com Phone: 786-575-6814 --- **Dear CARF Accreditation Review Committee,** We are submitting this formal complaint against **New Hope C.O.R.P.S.** for egregious violations of CARF’s accreditation standards, HIPAA-related confidentiality norms, and the core ethics of client service delivery.

Before detailing our complaint, it is important to explain why this letter is addressed to **CARF** instead of the **Miami-Dade County Homeless Trust**—the entity that funds and oversees New Hope. ### Why CARF and Not the Homeless Trust?

In a county where nonprofit funding is distributed based on politics, not performance, we no longer trust internal oversight mechanisms.

That’s why we’re appealing to you: an external accreditation body with professional and ethical authority. --- ### Summary of Violations (Full details in attached documentation) **1.

Illegal Eviction with No Legal Basis** New Hope staff posted an **unauthorized 5-day eviction notice** on private property using legal language only applicable to public land.

Ghosting, Obstruction, and Contradictions** Over the course of three years, multiple New Hope employees **promised housing, vanished without warning**, contradicted each other, and left us in limbo.

Retaliation for Truth** When we finally sent a diplomatic text to Stephen expressing our disappointment, he returned in person **shaking with rage**.

Florida Statutes Violated During Eviction and Property Destruction** --- ### **Florida Statute § 83.67 – Prohibited Acts by Landlord** > **“A landlord of any dwelling unit governed by this part shall not cause, directly or indirectly, the termination of utilities or services, or remove outside doors, locks, roof, or walls of the dwelling unit except as provided by law.”** **Violation:** New Hope, in coordination with MDPD and Code Compliance, initiated and participated in a **self-help eviction** without any court process—directly violating this statute. --- ### **Florida Statute § 82.045 – Unlawful Detainer / Ejectment Process** > **“The person entitled to possession of real property must seek a writ of possession via civil court before removing an occupant who does not have a lease.”** **Violation:** The eviction from the Chase Bank lot involved **no civil court case**, no summons, and no writ of possession.

The notice was **fabricated using irrelevant statutes** related to public land. --- ### **Florida Statute § 810.08 – Trespass in a Structure or Conveyance** > **“Trespass is a criminal offense only when notice is given by an authorized owner or agent under lawful authority.”** **Violation:** The 5-day notice was posted by **Stephen Heath**, who was not an owner, police officer, or legal agent of the property.

Legal Violations and Aftermath** The eviction was not just unethical—it was illegal under Florida law.

The actions of New Hope, MDPD, and Code Compliance collectively violated multiple statutes designed to protect both property rights and due process: #### **Key Violated Statutes:** - **F.S. § 83.67**: Prohibits “self-help” eviction tactics, including the removal of personal belongings or changing of locks without a court order. - **F.S. § 82.045**: Requires civil court proceedings for **ejectment or unlawful detainer** in any case involving removal from private property. - **F.S. § 810.08**: Defines trespass as a criminal offense only when proper, **lawful notice** has been served—**not when a notice is fabricated by unauthorized parties**.

What you’re describing gets to the heart of **a growing problem with how discretionary enforcement is being used—and abused—in Florida**, particularly when it comes to **trespassing laws** and **the criminalization of homelessness**.

Did Florida Law Eliminate the Need for a Signed Trespass Warning?** **No, the law didn’t exactly change—it’s just being interpreted in more dangerous ways.** There is no Florida statute that requires a **signed paper** as the *only* valid method to issue a trespass warning.

But in recent years, particularly in South Florida, police departments have shifted toward **verbal warnings**, or **documented verbal trespass warnings** (e.g., written by the officer, not signed by the individual).

Why That’s a Problem** What this effectively does is **weaponize vagueness**: - **You say you were never warned.** - **The cop says you were.** - No signature, no timestamp, no evidence either way. **In court, the officer’s word is often treated as truth—by default.** This creates a system that **presumes the officer is honest** and **presumes you are not**—which is unconstitutional in spirit and dangerous in practice. --- ### **3.

(implying a hidden motive or corruption) 3. **Deus ex machina** – "God from the machine" (used to criticize improbable or forced solutions) 4. **Quod erat demonstrandum (Q.E.D.)** – "Which was to be demonstrated" (often used sarcastically after proving an obvious point) 5. **Vox populi, vox Dei** – "The voice of the people is the voice of God" (used cynically to mock populism or mob mentality) 6. **Fiat lux** – "Let there be light" (mocking sudden, dramatic enlightenment) 7. **Habeas corpus** – "You shall have the body" (can be used cynically to demand accountability, often when someone shirks responsibility) 8. **Sic semper tyrannis** – "Thus always to tyrants" (mocking downfall or retribution against power-abusing figures) 9. **Non sequitur** – "It does not follow" (used to ridicule illogical conclusions or arguments) 10. **Memento mori** – "Remember you must die" (used to deliver a sharp reminder of mortality or the futility of vanity) 11. **Pax vobiscum** – "Peace be with you" (often used ironically in hostile situations) 12. **Sine qua non** – "Without which not" (mocking something that's absurdly or unnecessarily presented as essential) 13. **Panem et circenses** – "Bread and circuses" (used to criticize distractions used to placate the public) 14. **Ignorantia juris non excusat** – "Ignorance of the law excuses no one" (especially potent when used to target authority figures who misapply the law) Let me know which ones stand out to you and how you'd like to incorporate them! > **User (2025-02-09 19:53:09 UTC):** > > Use as many of these in the text where they apply and make it a sort of running theme as a critique of my own words as if hearing them spoken aloud in my head made me have a sudden epiphany which caused me to exclaim **Assistant (2025-02-11 16:05:05 UTC):** ### **Subject:** Request for Police Assistance with Special Circumstance Solution Dear Sergeant Byars, Respect isn’t earned by whining.
